# Zenon Taproot Puzzle Master Solve

**TminusZ**

**Research record through 6 November 2026**

## Preface

This book brings the Zenon treasure hunt and the subsequent investigation into one readable record. It begins with the public puzzle, explains the Bitcoin artwork and the authenticated first solution, and follows the later work through signatures, historical documents, poems, image construction, geometry, and competing readers. It preserves the experiments that failed as well as those that succeeded. A correction changes the conclusion attached to a result; it does not erase the result or the path that led to it.

The original treasure objective has a reproducible solution. The later investigation has established a substantial body of exact arithmetic, source relationships, geometric mechanisms, and bounded negative results. A complete authenticated secondary message has not been recovered. Those conclusions can coexist: the first solve is secure, and the later research contains real findings whose intended use is still being investigated. (B:Authenticated primary solve; [R1815](#source-r1815))

The readable account and the preservation collection serve different purposes. The book explains the investigation. The collection retains the exact reconstructed source texts, data, scripts, reports, figures, and package contents needed to inspect it. The source catalogue connects the two. The evidence can be read without continuing this conversation or trusting a model's recollection.

## The present result

The strongest results fall into several distinct groups.

**The public treasure has been solved.** Nineteen Bitcoin transaction payloads form a 19 by 38 character artwork. Four visible Base64 fields provide an AES-GCM nonce, ciphertext, and authentication tag. With the key derived from the exact word `Taproot`, the ciphertext authenticates and yields `wall nation drive smoke`. Together with the preserved eight-word prefix, this gives the public twelve-word quest mnemonic and its native Zenon account. (B:Authenticated primary solve; [V01](#v01-editorial-verification))

**The construction has a common signing authority.** The retained native verification attributes forty construction-signature events to public key Q. The terminal transaction commits to `ZENON NETWORK`. This proves a relationship between the construction and its signing key. It does not identify the human or organization controlling that key. (B:K09; B:Authenticated primary solve)

**The artwork has reproducible internal structure.** Its punctuation, q/1 markers, empty regions, repeated tiles, and mirrored boundaries support exact descriptions. A four-character `qzzq` move joins two original blank regions into a fifty-five-cell region. The original lower opening maps into the upper opening under a specified half-turn, leaving eleven upper roof cells unmatched. These calculations can be checked directly against the published characters. ([V01](#v01-editorial-verification); [R1674](#source-r1674))

**The parity readers have a precise information limit.** Three pairs of different printed glyphs give the same retained LOW/FULL code: z/comma, 1/semicolon, and q/period. Across the artwork these account for 285 literal choices. Adding the original ASCII bit-1 values resolves those choices when occupancy and the four native fields are retained. This identifies information discarded by the reader; it does not establish that all 285 choices were independently authored message bits. ([V01](#v01-editorial-verification); [R1803](#source-r1803))

**Several later candidate readers are coherent but remain inferred.** The mirror-XOR construction producing FOLLOW GOLD retains all five variable choices in the latest thirty-two-pattern calibration family. It therefore depends on the particular glyph choices, rather than following inevitably from the silhouette alone. Its input relocation, operation, and serialization still require an independently grounded selection rule. ([R1815](#source-r1815))

**Historical-authority claims remain a separate question.** The 145 historical Bitcoin-message signatures, OpenPGP certificate signatures, and public-key calculations have their own valid mathematical or native relationships. None of those results, by itself, proves that historical Satoshi controlled Q, that the historical-message publisher constructed the artwork, or that a later symbolic SATOSHI label is an external signature of authorship. (B:K24-K27; B:Current proof gaps and supersession rules)

## How to use this book

Read the opening account and the historical narrative first for the original problem and its solution. Continue into the October developments and the visual investigation to understand how the research changed. The technical reference preserves the detailed October atlas, including constants and complete branch descriptions. The correction ledger explains which parts of that older atlas require a narrower present reading.

Source labels are deliberately simple. **B** identifies a section of the retained October 13 technical atlas. **S** identifies one of its reconstructed source records. **R** identifies a later recovered file. A package-member identifier adds **M** and a member number. **V01** identifies the independent offline checks performed while preparing this edition. The preservation collection includes the catalogues behind these labels.

An experiment can be completely reproducible without being a complete solution. For example, turning a selected shape may give an exact bit string. That establishes the output of the stated operation. It leaves a different question: what selected that shape, turn, direction, and interpretation before the output was seen? The book keeps both questions visible so a useful calculation is neither lost nor overstated.

Dates identify the evidence being discussed. A later report is not automatically more reliable; its new source, counterexample, or corrected calculation must explain why it changes an earlier conclusion. Several reports contain the words complete, verified, proof, or breakthrough in their historical titles. Those titles are retained for identification. The current conclusions depend on the substance and controls, not the title.
