## 2. CONTROL98 and Z192: an exact capacity match, then a proposed installation

The original picture has 193 literal z characters, but one belongs to the Base64 text `4Fdzw1k=`. Masking only the four Base64 spans leaves 192 structural z positions. The same masked artwork has 98 punctuation/q/1 controls: 58 punctuation marks and forty q/1 glyphs. These are distinct populations of original source cells. ([R0086](#source-r0086))

For each control character, the retained LOW/FULL reader measures the parity of its low hexadecimal nibble and the parity of the full ASCII byte. The two-bit results are `:`→00, `,`→01, `.`/`q`→10, and `;`/`1`→11. Ninety-eight observations therefore give 196 bits. The first 192 form a candidate body; the final four are `1001`, which match the leading four SHA256 bits of that body. A standard eighteen-word BIP39 representation needs six checksum bits, so its last two checksum bits are calculated. They are not additional bits printed in the artwork. ([R0086](#source-r0086); [R1117](#source-r1117))

The original checksum-completed display is:

```text
ZOO SHRUG CLICK COOL PLANET START
UPDATE RAZOR FORUM PROVIDE FRESH RANGE
UNIFORM DAD FOOT EFFORT OFFER FRESH
```

This is an exact representation of the chosen source projection. Calling it an eighteen-word hidden plaintext would overstate the result. The attractive next hypothesis was to place the 192 body bits onto the 192 original z positions as a working memory. The capacity fits perfectly, but a literal z does not itself declare a writable binary cell. ([R0086](#source-r0086); [R1117](#source-r1117))

### 2.1 The historical panel and its orientation

The historical address source contains 145 rows of twenty-four bytes after removal of each Base58Check version byte. Reading a column as eleven-bit groups uses 143 rows and leaves two bits. Across twenty-four columns, those final two records provide a forty-eight-bit panel. In original source-column order, inverted least-significant bits give: ([R0089](#source-r0089))

```text
record143: 011011100100010100101001
record144: 010001001001000011100000
combined:  6e45294490e0  (hexadecimal)
```

The artwork has one complete two-row, twenty-four-column z band: zero-based rows 9–10, columns 7–30. In the row-major structural-z list, it is exactly `Z[44:92)`. The retained reverse-column paired reading of the historical bits gives LION PROOF BANNER FOX, followed by four residual bits `1100`. The four words pass the original eight-word-prefix BIP39 check. Subtracting ART102 gives INSTALL PANEL AIRPORT EXHIBIT; AIRPORT's index is 44. The residual `1100` belongs to the panel, and is separate from the checksum bits already contained in the FOX word. ([R0089](#source-r0089))

Installing the source rows in their original coordinate order at Z44 changes five displayed words to HOUR CITIZEN EYE CANYON ARM. The resulting memory retains the original four-bit checksum prefix `1001`; the calculated full six-bit prefix becomes `100111`. Its final displayed word changes FRESH→FRIEND because the two calculated checksum bits change. No original Bitcoin byte is edited. ([R0089](#source-r0089); [R0098](#source-r0098))

The orientation correction is consequential. The older placement that produced COMIC AUTHOR fails this original four-bit checksum. The newly retained placement produces a different eighty-eight-bit strip, with its ART-shifted words INSTALL WINTER STUFF MARGIN AIRPORT AUNT EXPLAIN FETCH. COMIC AUTHOR and EYE CANYON cannot both be claimed as confirmation of the same installed orientation. ([R0086](#source-r0086); [R0089](#source-r0089))

### 2.2 What the finite tests add

The full 20,880 ordered distinct historical-record pairs were examined under four frozen checksum conditions. Individual gates admitted 1,261, 1,293, 1,152 and 1,296 pairs; all four together admitted only `(143,144)`. The conditions included the original CONTROL checksum, the panel's checked four-word reading, and two WIDTH-shifted windows ending at the affected span's boundaries. The pair is specific within that selected family. The gates were identified during an already informed investigation and share source bits and hash contexts, so one surviving pair is not a global probability of authorial intent. ([R0100](#source-r0100))

The installation also returned to existing EYE/CANYON and CHECK AXIS paths. Those paths led back to native Q verification and block 709632. They formed useful consistency loops, rather than introducing a new secret or another funded prize. The tempting new completion HOUR CITIZEN EYE CANYON fails the ordinary twelve-word checksum. ([R0098](#source-r0098); [R0100](#source-r0100))

## 3. GLASS: eleven bits moving through three kinds of source cell

GLASS names a displayed word in a conditional working state. Its actual eleven logical bits are `01011101101`, decimal 749. Under ordinary BIP39 numbering, 749 is FROZEN; the retained WIDTH-shifted view adds 42 and displays GLASS791. Those bits were not eleven q/1 characters printed in the original artwork. Their donor positions were originally spaces. Keeping logical contents, displayed labels and original source characters separate became essential. ([R0246](#source-r0246))

The chamber geometry itself is exact. The upper blank component has thirty-three cells: an eleven-cell roof and rows of nine, seven, four and two cells. Rotating the final twenty-two about `(15,18.5)` maps them exactly to the lower twenty-two-cell staircase, by `(r,c)→(30−r,37−c)`. Only row pivot 15 gives a complete match in the tested row-pivot family. This gives a concrete candidate meaning for the two FOLDs, while the instruction selecting that action remains part of the conditional interpretation. ([R0143](#source-r0143))

The complete GLASS translation uses rows+6 and columns−11. Its eleven destinations divide without remainder into five original lower-chamber blanks, four original z cells, and two original CONTROL owners. The four z cells are Z181–184; the overwritten controls are the comma at `(18,6)` and q at `(18,11)`. A border-only projection misses nine physical changes and cannot substitute for this full move. ([R0230](#source-r0230); [R0250](#source-r0250))

### 3.1 RAZOR/NATURE tests and the PROBLEM return

The four Base64 fields have residual lengths 7|1|7|7 when each is read separately as complete eleven-bit values. Their concatenated twenty-two bits are RAZOR/NATURE. The ASCII LOW/FULL representation of the conditional GLASS glyphs is ROOM/TERM. Across all twenty-two bits, GLASS AND residual equals the unchanged residual; XOR gives ANNOUNCE/EQUAL. These are full-width identities, but no recovered local instruction binds those operands to AND or XOR. ([R0246](#source-r0246))

The controls also bound the apparent selectivity. Thirty aligned windows of the original forty-glyph carrier produce no containment match, but 35 of the 330 eleven-position strings with GLASS's four-q population do. Two cyclic residual rotations preserve containment. The exact relationship is worth retaining; it is not a twenty-two-bit authenticator. ([R0246](#source-r0246))

Installing RAZOR/NATURE in the lower chamber and applying the full GLASS move changes it to RAZOR/PROBLEM. Five chamber destinations are covered; two retain their previous bits and three change. PROBLEM1371 minus NATURE1179 equals 192, the structural-z count. The output rejoins the already known numbered-poem/P2095 branch. It does not create eleven newly verified bits or an independent permission to dispatch into that branch. Fifteen of 330 fixed-weight donors and two of eleven donor rotations give the same result. ([R0250](#source-r0250))

The same move writes `1110` over Z181–184, replacing `0010` in the installed memory. Its calculated SHA prefix changes from `100111` to `000100`, and the displayed last words change OFFER FRIEND→PANDA FLAT. A matching four-bit CONTROL prefix provides a conditional cross-layer check. It does not establish PANDA or FLAT as instructions. ([R0230](#source-r0230); [R0250](#source-r0250))

### 3.2 CONTROL96 was a useful failed interpretation

Removing the two overwritten CONTROL owners deletes `0110` from the original 196-bit record and leaves 192 bits. However, the last four of those bits are still the old checksum. The survivor is therefore **188 retained body bits plus four retained tag bits**, rather than a newly authenticated 192-bit entropy object. Hashing it as fresh entropy gives `0111`, which fails both the old footer `1001` and the removed nibble `0110`. ([R0246](#source-r0246); [R0248](#source-r0248))

The checksum delta `1001 XOR 0111 = 1110` matches GLASS's four deposited z bits. Yet fourteen of ninety-seven adjacent-owner deletions give that delta, and two both remove `0110` and give the delta. Four fixed attempts to use GLASS's deposited bits as a replacement for the removed bits all fail the old checksum. Recomputing a new BIP39 checksum would always create a valid representation; it would not recover an authored message. ([R0248](#source-r0248))

This work established a practical distinction between immutable source, a working image and saved parent state. No two-cell parity replacement made every original FILE and working check pass simultaneously. Later return tests showed that live copyback, cached restoration and a saved-snapshot return can agree on the currently required observations. The accepted donor does not uniquely select a physical COPYBACK or POP operation. ([R0230](#source-r0230); [R0316](#source-r0316))

## 4. PULL TWENTY becomes PIPE TWENTY before witness verification

The working-state names need a precise map. **PRIVATE33** is the original upper thirty-three-cell blank component, numbered from zero in row-major order. Its ordinal 20 is the twenty-first cell, at `(13,19)`; GLASS occupies ordinals 9–19 before MOVE. **FULL55** reads the larger rectangle at rows 11–15, columns 15–25: the thirty-three private cells plus twenty-two surrounding cells. It takes the current working characters' FULL parity in row-major order, groups the bits into five eleven-bit values, and applies the retained WIDTH shift, equivalent to adding 42 modulo 2048. The separate lower twenty-two-cell chamber is outside this rectangle. These readers retain original source coordinates while their working contents change. ([R0748](#source-r0748); [R0326](#source-r0326); [R0340](#source-r0340))

A scheduling correction made the proposed program more intelligible. Earlier reconstruction restored private bit 20 before moving GLASS. The corrected replay moved GLASS first, leaving that bit unchanged until a checked instruction requested its restoration. Both schedules give the same final working image, but the revised order exposes a meaningful intermediate state. ([R0340](#source-r0340))

Checksum pairs below show stored/computed four-bit values in decimal: `6/6` means both equal binary `0110`. ([R0340](#source-r0340))

| Working phase | Full fifty-five-bit display | Relevant result |
|---|---|---|
| Before MOVE | DRILL GLASS PULL TWENTY DEVOTE | This full display's last-four checksum fails; the separately compacted parent has its own valid check. |
| After MOVE | DRIP LUNCH PULL TWENTY DEVOTE | Last-four checksum passes, 6/6. |
| Restore private ordinal20 at `(13,19)` | DRIP LUNCH PIPE TWENTY DEVOTE | One bit changes PULL1386 to PIPE1322; checksum remains 6/6. |
| Complete final punctuation | Same five words | Working CONTROL passes 11/11 and frame/chamber agree at PROBLEM1371. |

The complete five-field reading is `!9 / B9 / R10 / u9 / RS6`, using a seven-bit ASCII field plus four-bit immediate. A proposed human interpretation reads nine unsigned bits beginning at absolute column 9 of row 10. That gives `000100100`, decimal 36. The terminal has nineteen two-item witness stacks, so flat item 36 is input18's signature, followed by Q at item37. The signature genuinely verifies in its original Bitcoin domain. ([R0326](#source-r0326); [R0340](#source-r0340))

Several tempting shortcuts were tested rather than accepted. Boolean NOT of 36 gives zero, but so does NOT of any nonzero value, including an invalid uninstalled locator. Rotating 36 right by ten within nine bits gives 18 because that rotation is equivalent to one place; it is not independent confirmation. Nor can R10 simultaneously supply the physical row and an unrelated rotation without an additional rule. ([R0326](#source-r0326))

The later bitwise-complement interpretation is more discriminating. Nine-bit NOT of 36 is 475, or `11101101|1`. Its leading byte ED matches GLASS's low eight bits; the inverted final address bit selects signature item0. Frozen controls reject the alternate in-range window28, the public-key sibling37 and the no-panel value466. This is a useful cross-representation constraint, while the meaning of `!9` and the witness namespace remain inferred. ([R0340](#source-r0340))

The independent October17 audit found native terminal18 `r32` word4=749 and `s32` word15=2006, each unique among the respective 437 complete scalar cells in the nineteen signatures. Its selected `s17−s15` gives PROBLEM1371, but four directed pairs across the wider s-field census give that difference. The complete 182-byte BIP143 preimage verifies under Q. The associated ED/36/38 coinbase pattern comes from standard BIP141 witness-commitment framing; those properties cannot be counted as three independent rare markers. ([R0352](#source-r0352))

Following PIPE TWENTY into the companion key's twenty-byte HASH160 gives a real protocol consumer: it recreates all nineteen parent P2WPKH scripts. Several alternatives fail. No version byte makes the original CONTROL payload, CONTROL96 survivor or final CONTROL a valid stripped Base58Check address. The PULL and PIPE mnemonic candidates do not match the pinned genesis address population, whereas the authenticated original mnemonic does. These checks return to the established construction rather than proving a second reward. ([R0342](#source-r0342))

## 5. Historical mask selection and the source-only replay

The historical path uses the final published signature's native s scalar. At positions 7,11,15,19 it gives LYRICS SCRIPT PATTERN LIST, with a valid four-word-prefix check. LYRICS1067 can be split into ASCII B and index11. Among the four field-local decoded streams, only B has enough complete eleven-bit cells for index11; B[11] is PUT1398. GLOBAL40[11] is RECALL instead, so the field-local namespace matters. The letter B is an analyst's field label, which is another part of the proposed grammar. ([R0207](#source-r0207))

The remaining SCRIPT/PATTERN/LIST values supply a thirty-three-bit mask. Its established geometric projection gives 58|19, matching punctuation count and artwork height. Among 145 fixed s-column packets, six pass the checksum and only the last record has that in-bounds typed head. Expanding to 1,160 R/S column cases produces fifty-eight checksum passes and two in-bounds typed heads. The narrow uniqueness must not be generalized beyond its census. LIST's low four bits already serve as the packet checksum; reusing them in the mask does not add four independent check bits. ([R0207](#source-r0207))

### 5.1 What “2021-only” actually achieved

The source-only panel construction derives HOUR/CITIZEN from byte widths and panel dimensions, EYE from the mirrored chamber, CANYON from the residual XOR, and ARM from another retained source relation. BOTTOM/COVER/ADDRESS supply a proposed table blueprint. Their high-seven parts sum to artwork width38, and their low-four parts sum to legacy address size25; only one of 9,880 GLOBAL40 triples has both totals. These are exact finite results, but the field splits, selected arithmetic and panel formulas were developed after the historical match was known. ([R0295](#source-r0295))

A separate donor construction derives four GLASS bits from a bottom-Z comparison and five from the proposed RAZOR→PROBLEM target, then resolves the remaining two CONTROL bits and final punctuation through retained checks. One of 8,192 donor/punctuation combinations passes: donor749 and semicolon. The four physical predicates reflect the five-blank/four-z/two-control partition. They do not make 1/8,192 a probability of intention. ([R0321](#source-r0321))

The isolated runtime then omitted the 145-address file altogether and rebuilt the candidate panel, donor, PULL→PIPE sequence, witness36 and final working image. This was a genuine removal of a runtime dependency. The full five-field grammar remained learned from prior research. Among 2,097,152 allowed private-memory states, 348 passed all three phase checks; twenty-six also matched B9/R10/u9/RS6; one additionally matched the exact `!9` head. A looser checksum test alone therefore did not discover the program. ([R0344](#source-r0344))

The source-only mask comparison reproduced thirty-two of the historical mask's thirty-three bits. Its uncorrected labels were SCRIPT PATTERN LESSON; restoring the previously identified bit gave SCRIPT PATTERN LIST. That agreement is preserved as an exact correspondence. Software isolation cannot turn the method's already informed construction into a historically blind discovery. ([R0344](#source-r0344))

### 5.2 October27 extended the comparison, without making it a signature

The later historical-source audit compared all 290 r/s halves under the fixed mask-to-donor recipe: only record144's s produced donor749. Across 874 complete r cells in the thirty-eight row/terminal Q signatures, terminal18 r[4] alone contained 749. The following native cell r[5] is PATTERN1290, also the middle word of the historical mask. ([R1090](#source-r1090); [R1092](#source-r1092))

This gives a contiguous 2021 pair `(749,1290)`, but the historical pair is hybrid: a reconstructed donor plus a raw mask field. It is not a direct twenty-two-bit copy of a continuous historical signature interval. The donor calculation already uses some PATTERN bits. Wider controls show that 749 also occurs in terminal16's s scalar, while PATTERN occurs only once across all 1,748 scalar cells. Longer direct-copy tests fail in the declared families. ([R1090](#source-r1090); [R1092](#source-r1092))

A proposed 2021 selector connects PLATE/PIECE, a physical PRIMARY|4 read and the transformed border NEXT PRIMARY STEM PRINT to terminal18's primary DER integer r and fields4/5. The same wording also has a plausible physical-stem interpretation. Choosing DER, the meaning of NEXT, and the historical publication still need a source rule. Neither the eleven-bit donor match nor the extended pair authenticates a common historical controller. ([R1092](#source-r1092))

## 6. E15, C5 and ORBIT: native continuations with unresolved types

The October18 typed-interpreter review reproduced seventy-five passing tests and an independently checked native input4 proof. Its program counter still ended at `UNRESOLVED_POST_VERIFY_HEAD`. Fresh modules integrated inherited routines; that was a new implementation and a useful direct path, not independent discovery of the instruction language. ([R0482](#source-r0482))

One continuation begins at input4: r[2] gives `s12`, then s[12] gives `i15`. Two coherent candidate meanings of `i15` were preserved. Treating it as a different input leads through input15's r and DER fields to Q.y[14]=SATOSHI1531. Treating it as field15 of the current forty-one-byte input serialization gives E15, then the original authentication tag byte E[15]=27, ADDRESS. Native signatures verify in both relevant contexts. The choice of receiver is the unresolved step. A SATOSHI dictionary value in Q is not a historical signature. ([R0486](#source-r0486))

E[15] and GLOBAL40[39] both equal 27 because they share the same final tag bits. They are not independent discoveries. Reading G40[27] gives MAMMAL1077, or `C5` under the proposed character/nibble grammar. A literal zero-based C[5] byte read is out of bounds: C has five bytes. Interpreting C/5 as a length assertion succeeds and returns to the original AES-GCM envelope, but requires an explicitly different type. The successful tag check cannot retroactively choose that type. ([R0486](#source-r0486); [R0487](#source-r0487))

Here **SIDE** names a 132-bit record: read the current LOW/FULL values at the first fifty-seven original punctuation-owner coordinates, giving 114 bits, then append the eighteen bits of the unchanged `glP` Base64 sextets from tag E. The resulting twelve eleven-bit fields retain those fixed source owners even when a working character changes class. ([R0470](#source-r0470))

ORBIT offered a more source-local selector. B[9]=22 and the retained SIDE field FINE=`+6` give index28. G40[28] is ORBIT1247 at the genuine C/E field boundary, with split `9|95`. The 95 also equals ASCII q XOR period. Thus the changed punctuation owner can be viewed through a read-only parity projection without physically rewriting q into period. A physical rewrite breaks the live q/1 type even when some other checks survive. ([R0470](#source-r0470); [R0489](#source-r0489))

Under another candidate type assignment, tag9 selects input9 and B's eighteen-byte length selects r[18]. That field and TxIn[21] form a reciprocal GLOBAL40-checked loop, unique among 437 starts in the fixed census. It returns AIRPORT44, the known panel location. The forty-eight-bit panel comparison succeeds, but its width comes from the existing assembly; the loop itself supplies only the start. ([R0487](#source-r0487); [R0489](#source-r0489))

The decisive phase test found that this loop and panel equality already work after MOVE, before full VERIFY. They therefore cannot authorize a post-verification jump by themselves. A software gate that grants permission after VERIFY is sensible implementation discipline; it is not recovery of the missing authored instruction. ([R0489](#source-r0489))

A separate native selector did identify the original prize allocation: WEALTH1985 combined with terminal18 AMOUNT64 gives the low eighteen amount bits, uniquely matching the prize among 14,091 balance entries; only one of seventy-six fixed punctuation/signature combinations matches. These are token balances within 14,090 account records: 14,088 QSR balances and three ZNN balances. However, the final punctuation packet carrying WEALTH fails its complete twelve-word checksum. The individual field remains real source data, while permission to consume it in that phase stays conditional. ([R0405](#source-r0405))

## 7. E1 and `70|5`: a concrete signature-format correspondence

The October21 EYES→FOLLOWING→IN→AT route selected two aligned blocks from the original E authentication tag. Its left block E[33:66), folded through the established chamber map, gives: ([R0614](#source-r0614))

```text
CODE PLATE PIECE SEVEN MILLION
22|8  S1    R3    b5    F5
```

The first four words pass the original-prefix BIP39 check, 5/5. MILLION1125 is outside that check. It splits into 70|5, displayed as F5. The repeated low-five values in b5 and F5 are not independent confirmations: the fold reverses PIECE and PLATE, which share the source bits that produce those suffixes. Moreover, b5's low nibble already serves as the checksum. ([R0614](#source-r0614))

By October24, a native candidate receiver had been found. Interpret `70|5` as seventy-byte DER length and byte offset5, and compare against the boundary value95. Among all thirty-eight original row-publisher and terminal signatures, only E's own row publisher satisfies both. Its transaction is `57b5f224beb471fa78caeca665229166ec96da7798e5d05b15e37c07276a2476`; its native BIP143 signature verifies under Q. The real publisher-to-terminal outpoint then leads to terminal input8, rather than an arbitrary row selected for its output. ([R0741](#source-r0741); [R0830](#source-r0830))

The serialization experiment also works. E's final tag byte27 plus the candidate nibble5 gives compact header32, which recovers Q from the original r, s and transaction digest. This is a conversion of representation, not a new signature over text. Header32 denotes compressed legacy P2PKH recovery; a P2WPKH message-signing interpretation would use header40 for the same recovery identifier. That address-family switch still needs authorization. ([R0830](#source-r0830))

High-S controls distinguish the mined representation: replacing s by n−s preserves valid ECDSA but changes DER length70→71 and requires header31 to recover Q. Header32 alone is common—twenty-five of thirty-eight original signatures use it in the legacy representation, and fifteen combine it with DER length70. The extra byte5=95 condition supplies the source-specific distinction. ([R0830](#source-r0830))

The publisher/spender/sweep byte arithmetic also returns characters of the already known ZENON NETWORK output. It preserves genuine transaction ancestry and exact values, but no new Boolean opcode, message or callback is established. The useful result is a narrower, testable source-format correspondence. ([R0741](#source-r0741))

## 8. The poems gained a source chronology, not automatic authority

The Crypto Publius dossier first assembled screenshots and then a 148-record timeline export. The export distinguishes 138 own-account URLs from ten quote/repost rows pointing to others. Its 2021 own-account subset contains twenty-seven posts, twenty-five before the artwork. All 148 timestamp values agree with decoded tweet IDs when the export is interpreted in America/Toronto, rather than UTC. This corrected several apparent calendar discrepancies. ([R0554](#source-r0554); [R0633](#source-r0633))

The original [official March5 binary post](https://x.com/Zenon_Network/status/1367883812764590081) reads Z3E2N1ON. Publius's [map post](https://x.com/crypto_publius/status/1367887580277178368) follows 898 seconds later. It shows contemporary participation in Zenon's encoded-clue culture; it does not make Publius the author of the official binary or specify a 3–2–1 decoder for the later Bitcoin artwork. ([R0633](#source-r0633))

The [July SMOKE post](https://x.com/crypto_publius/status/1419503629778620416) is July26 at 03:43:33 UTC despite its local July25 display. Its “beneath the smoke” language has a precise possible referent: the already separated five-byte C ciphertext corresponds to the plaintext tail smoke, and its narrow printed span lies above the q-run while excluding the parallel one-run. The other completion words WALL, NATION and DRIVE occur zero times in the 138 own-account posts. The evidence is one source-specific alignment, not advance publication of the whole solution. ([R0633](#source-r0633))

The [October26 poem](https://x.com/crypto_publius/status/1452886881373466626) emphasizes ends, small portions, truth/lies and following one's eyes. The April19 and October26 posts are the only own-account posts containing both eyes and deceive, with opposed advice. Meanwhile, June25 supplies a necessary control: it also has fifty words. The same frozen five lookups give REVELATION TO BOLD BUT MY in June and TRUTH SEPARATE EYES FROM DECEIVE in October. Word count alone therefore cannot select the latter. Direct line-initial readings across the poems yield no obvious instruction. ([R0554](#source-r0554); [R0633](#source-r0633))

The visual test added FOLLOW GOLD by reading the same mirrored-chamber XOR bits right-to-left within each row; the older left-to-right reading remains ONE EYE. This offers a possible link to the earlier Golden Record quest. All six tested combinations of RAZOR NATURE, ONE EYE and FOLLOW GOLD as alternative four-word mnemonic endings fail. The words can be studied as instructions, but they are not another authenticated seed completion. ([R0551](#source-r0551))

On October27 the source-selection proposal became more concrete: RAZOR/NATURE beside CONTROL's RAZOR/FORUM, conditional subtraction to SIGN, the 145-address blueprint, the poem's truth/lies/end language, and the earlier official “we are all Satoshi” context point toward the [May2020 signed forum publication](https://bitcointalk.org/index.php?topic=5250960.0). This is a plausible human lookup route, with documented alternatives and context. The arithmetic, publication choice and parsing were still recognized during research; no literal 2021 commitment to that exact historical publication was recovered. ([R1090](#source-r1090))

## 9. Late-October corrections: which reading survives which phase?

The original CONTROL display really contains UPDATE RAZOR FORUM PROVIDE FRESH RANGE. October28 tested two different readings instead of merging their advantages. An internal SUB-based reduction produces FRESH REPAIR BEGIN DEVICE with a valid original-prefix checksum. Across sixty-six fixed window/operator cases, eight outputs pass, so that window is not isolated by its checksum. A proposed replacement fails until its checksum is recomputed, which generates rather than discovers the repaired final word. ([R1117](#source-r1117))

The external panel overwrite preserves the original CONTROL four-bit prefix but changes the overlapping source words. Under the same internal SUB grammar, its RANGE child becomes FRESH REPAIR BEGIN DEVELOP and fails. A consumed-before-update interpretation is possible, but a source rule must establish that ordering and saved state. The internal and external readings are not two simultaneous confirmations. Directly loading either suggestive thirty-three-bit phrase into the upper cavity also fails the required seven-q-stem pattern; none of sixty-four fixed consecutive-window/orientation variants supplies it. ([R1117](#source-r1117))

E2 supplied another useful challenge. Its candidate matcher recognizes `q,` followed by nine spaces on the untouched original artwork. After E1 loading, clearing only those nine cells makes it pass again while retaining the other twenty-four E1 cells. Thus it can be an original-state recognizer or a local vacancy check; it does not establish that the entire E1 object moved. ([R1142](#source-r1142))

The October26 renderer likewise has two complementary results. Authentic Base64 strings plus a frozen, previously inferred Z/N grammar recreate all 722 original cells. A hostile forty-two-glyph substitution then preserves the complete symbol census, occupied positions, CONTROL coordinates, all 196 parity bits and AES-GCM plaintext, while changing the original artwork identity. The two parity observations cannot recover glyph ownership alone. The full renderer is a sufficient fitted reconstruction, not recovered historical production code. ([R1003](#source-r1003))

By October29, a source-only shading test found 38 of forty q/1 marks and all fifty-eight punctuation marks on declared shape edges, against seventy-two of 192 z cells. The result supports an ordinary image-shading explanation, while leaving deliberate reuse as a possible data channel. A contemporary solver had already proposed an image-to-ASCII generator. The tested converter repertoire contains the observed characters, but neither the actual original input image nor an exact historical conversion was established in that report. ([R1173](#source-r1173))

These results narrow the October boundary. The older P00=MERGE boot remains a separate hypothesis: improved physical replay does not retroactively make that transformed word an opcode. The physical program has reproducible states and real native verification, while its source-issued grammar and post-VERIFY instruction remain unresolved. The next chapters carry the geometry, rendering and source investigations forward without losing either the successful October computations or the controls that limited them. (B:K04; [R0482](#source-r0482); [R1003](#source-r1003); [R1173](#source-r1173))


# Part III — The artwork and the visual investigation

## 1. What the picture contains, and what a visual result means

The artwork is a small, exact object: nineteen rows, each thirty-eight ASCII characters wide. Its spaces matter as much as its printed characters. The separator-free 722-byte source has SHA-256 `869020c3e598955bfc71244923c1fb557a5e0d4cadd89443012c8232bfa618d7`. A copy with an LF after every row has 741 bytes and needs its own hash. The on-chain source establishes the character sheet; an official PNG establishes a particular later visual rendering. Those are related sources with different commitments. (B:K11; B:K34; [R1696](#source-r1696))

The full-sheet accounting is:

| Population | Cells |
|---|---:|
| Outer margins | 228 |
| Four Base64 fields | 80 |
| Punctuation | 58 |
| Structural q/1 markers | 40 |
| z shading | 192 |
| Interior spaces | 124 |
| **Total** | **722** |

The 58 punctuation characters include thirty internal marks and the twenty-eight-character outer frame. Structural q/1 enumeration excludes q and 1 characters inside Base64 fields. Several later experiments use smaller regions: a 380-cell analysis region, a 120-cell canopy, a 98-character control roster, or particular blank components. Their counts describe different populations and must never silently replace the whole-sheet totals. (B:K11–K15; B:K19; B:K23)

All coordinates in this chapter are zero-based `(row,column)`. A *source owner* is a particular character at its original coordinate. Two z characters have the same appearance but different owners. If a character moves in an experimental scene, its owner follows it. A reflected image can display a source owner again; that is a second observation of existing information. A boundary can also touch one character on several faces. Counting every face is legitimate for a perimeter algorithm, but it does not create independent characters.

Three distinct accomplishments recur. A **source observation** identifies what is printed or visible. A **candidate reader** adds a rule for turning observations into bits, words, coordinates, or instructions. A **verified output** passes the relevant independent check. The primary AES-GCM solve has such authentication. Most visual continuations establish observations or reproducible readers, often with short checks, while their intended interpretation remains open. This distinction preserves promising work without turning every readable word into a command.

## 2. The custom punctuation scale and the outer frame

### Reading the four punctuation shapes

Remove punctuation in outer columns 6 and 31. The thirty remaining marks form upper and lower fifteen-character fields:

```text
upper .:..:,,;,,;,,;,
lower ;.,::;.,,.;:,.;
```

A first representation treats punctuation as two physical features: an extra upper dot and a lower tail. In that order period is `00`, comma `01`, colon `10`, and semicolon `11`. Four choices of field direction, with an axis-swap control, make eight specified candidates. The retained fixed-prefix BIP39 check selects upper-forward/lower-reverse in that family. It produces four eleven-bit words, each still attached to its four-bit suffix: EMPTY/2, AMONG/15, OBSERVE/9, PRIZE/5. (B:K12)

The suffixes then point into the same fifteen-mark traversal that produced each record. One-based indexing works for all four; zero-based indexing cannot address suffix 15 in a fifteen-position field. The selected symbols are colon, comma, period, semicolon. Assigning these ranks 0–3 produces the custom scale used throughout the later work:

| Punctuation | Custom bits | First-generation word/suffix | Selected original coordinate |
|---|---|---|---|
| `:` | `00` | EMPTY/2 | `(3,19)` |
| `,` | `01` | AMONG/15 | `(7,18)` |
| `.` | `10` | OBSERVE/9 | `(14,21)` |
| `;` | `11` | PRIZE/5 | `(16,17)` |

This is an exact, local construction. It is not a second AES authentication event. Reading the original fields under the resulting scale gives upper MEAT / ROAD with residual `01011101`, and lower TOP / COLLECT with residual `00011011`. Reading the two bit lanes in parallel gives RAISE/2, AMONG/15, SMOKE/3, PRIZE/5. Applying the same suffix consumer again selects colon, comma, comma, semicolon, or `00010111`. (B:K12; B:K35)

The lower residual `00011011` equals the scale alphabet encoded by itself. The three eight-bit values 93, 27, and 23 can be labelled ARM, ADDRESS, and ACTUAL in a dictionary-based display. Those labels do not themselves supply an operation that converts arbitrary eight-bit fragments into an executable word-machine state. Preserve TOP / COLLECT in its actual order; reversing it to “collect top” would add an unprovided instruction. (B:K12)

### What the frame explains

The outer frame contains six semicolons, one comma, one period, and twenty more commas. Under the custom scale the complete twenty-eight-mark stream gives:

| Reading | Complete eleven-bit values | Word labels | Remaining bits |
|---|---|---|---|
| Full frame | 2047, 1429, 682, 1365, 682 | ZOO / RAZOR / FETCH / PRIMARY / FETCH | `1` |
| XOR against all-comma carrier | 1365, 192, 0, 0, 0 | PRIMARY / BLOSSOM / ABANDON / ABANDON / ABANDON | `0` |
| Twenty-two-mark post-gap XOR | 384, 0, 0, 0 | COPY / ABANDON / ABANDON / ABANDON | none |

The all-comma baseline repeats `01`. Removing the six initial semicolons removes twelve bits, shifting eleven-bit grouping by one place. That explains why the period defect contributes 192 in the full alignment but 384 in the suffix alignment. The six semicolon substitutions contribute alternating bit positions and sum to 1365. The phase relationship is more informative than treating BLOSSOM and COPY as separate discoveries. (B:K13; B:K35)

A matching PRIMARY field and a missing `01` make a repair possible, but the frame alone does not identify a copy source, destination, or execution point. Its lower twenty marks leave seven residual bits `1010101` = 85. This is not a complete eleven-bit word. The equality `213 & 127 = 85` prevents using this residual as independent recovery of an old APPLE checkpoint. The proposed division `85 divmod 19 = (4,9)` remains an inferred coordinate adapter. (B:K13)

Later work also tested a Voyager analogy: fourteen rows have punctuation at both outer boundaries, but those rows contain only three pair types—three `;;`, one `,.`, and ten `,,`. NASA's fourteen pulsar directions carry individually encoded timing information. The matching number fourteen did not produce a source-defined clock, spoke origin, or pulsar reader for this artwork. ([R1780](#source-r1780))

## 3. q/1, LOW/FULL, and the 98-control record

### The original marker stream

Read structural q as 0 and digit 1 as 1 in original row-major order:

```text
upper18 100000100010000100
lower22 0101110101001100010000
full40  1000001000100001000101110101001100010000
```

There are twenty-seven q characters and thirteen ones. The upper eighteen contain four ones; the lower twenty-two contain nine. The exact upper slots were reconstructed by a bounded allocator: five first-inward pivots, twelve right-transition cells at stride three, and one phase-compensation cell. Occupied starts advance 2,3,3,3 rather than a uniform structural stride of three. The missing increment selects the compensation. This was the sole exact slot allocator in its thirty-five-candidate family; without compensation it found seventeen correct slots and missed one. It did not independently predict the marker values. (B:K14; B:K23)

A separate test tried using the forty bits to select the correct public-key recovery candidate from each of forty ECDSA construction signatures. Each signature had two recovery candidates, and all 780 pairwise intersections isolated Q. The native marker mask chose Q at only 22/40 positions, its complement at 18/40. The best native distance was thirteen, while controls reached eight; 7,960 of 10,000 same-weight random masks scored better. Replacing s by n−s preserves the recovery sets. This rejected that specified selector scheme, while leaving the physical marker geometry intact. (B:K14)

### A parity representation that preserves the punctuation scale

LOW/FULL reads two properties of each ASCII byte:

```text
LOW  = parity of ASCII's low four bits
FULL = parity of the complete ASCII byte
```

Write LOW first. This gives `:→00`, `,→01`, `.→10`, `;→11`, `q→10`, and `1→11`. Space and z also map to `01`. Taking the fifty-eight punctuation owners and forty structural q/1 owners in source order gives CONTROL98: 196 bits, interpreted as a 192-bit body and four stored final bits. Its exact original body and footer are:

```text
body   fff8e8aa97ea5fa97ba5955bb59d7358eed66dd6ba35996b
footer 1001
SHA256(body), first four bits: 1001
```

The match is real but short: four checksum bits. It is not a signature, not a full BIP39 entropy/checksum record, and not proof that this is the intended decoder. ([R0180](#source-r0180))

A conditional `f=3p` experiment projected only original q/1 controls from one-bit values to `0→00, 1→11`, retaining punctuation and source positions. It produced body `fff8e80017c05f0173059553b51d7350ced44dd4b035114b` and footer `0001`, which matched its own SHA-256 prefix. Twenty-six q LOW bits changed in the body and the final q at `(18,23)` changed in the footer. Pairing the original and projected bodies with both footers gave pass/fail/fail/pass, as expected for an actual state-sensitive check. ([R0180](#source-r0180))

Using the original upper-eighteen selector, the projected record yielded the checked conditional four-word return VETERAN SATISFY EXOTIC WHEAT after subtracting ART102. Only the second and fourth selected words changed; VETERAN and EXOTIC were already present in the baseline. In 200,000 count-preserving marker relabelings, 12,540 passed the projected SHA4 predicate (comparison with the first four SHA-256 bits), 12,623 passed the selected four-word check, and 739 passed both. These are results for the declared local null model, not author-intent probabilities. The equation's historical-source selection and the later inactive G0 entrypoint remained unresolved. ([R0180](#source-r0180))

## 4. The lower chamber, reflected memory, and older matrix branches

### A real empty shape; several proposed ways to load it

The lower blank chamber has consecutive row lengths 2,4,7,9 with a shared left boundary: row 15 columns 12–13, row 16 columns 12–15, row 17 columns 12–18, and row 18 columns 12–20. This is twenty-two spaces. The fixed search for that run-length pattern found one such chamber in the twenty-six-column interior. The shape is an observation; deciding which bits to place into it is a reader hypothesis. (B:K15; B:K35)

One early loading used the twenty-two concatenated field-local residual bits. Installed top-to-bottom and left-to-right, its rows are `10`, `1100`, `1010110`, and `010011011`. Reading outer and inner rows top-first gives NATURE / SKULL; bottom-first gives EVIDENCE / PULSE. Another loading reflects the chamber horizontally about the sheet's centre: the intersection contains four cells and the union forty. Vertical or double reflection gives union forty-four. The forty-marker stream can therefore be loaded into the horizontal forty-cell union in source order. (B:K15)

At each original chamber coordinate, compare the loaded bit with its reflected counterpart. In ordinary row-major output order the alternatives are XOR → ONE / EYE, XNOR → GRAB / PUT, OR → ONLINE / FARM, and AND → ABLE / ACOUSTIC. These are all retained. The mirrored memory contains relocated bits; the original blank chamber does not literally contain forty printed markers. That distinction becomes essential when comparing a direct optical view with a scratch-memory decode. (B:K15; [R1815](#source-r1815))

### The eight-glyph and matrix hypothesis

The candidate 380-cell region comprises upper rows 3–7 columns 7–30 and lower rows 9–18 columns 6–31. Its inventory is space 114, period 7, colon 5, comma 31, semicolon 7, z 176, ones 13, q 27. The proposed ordinal alphabet—space, period, colon, comma, semicolon, z, 1, q—differs from the custom punctuation order. It needs its own binding. Adjacent ordinal pairs divide the population into 121 space/period cells, 36 colon/comma, 183 semicolon/z, and forty 1/q. Within this model ART102 equals ordinal bit 1 XOR bit 0. (B:K19)

The 121- and 36-bit lanes reshape to candidate 11×11 and 6×6 matrices. Their preserved integer forms are:

```text
low rows     0,1536,1024,0,0,8,0,0,256,64,1
low columns  768,512,4,0,2,0,0,32,0,0,1
high rows    48,0,0,18,1,0
high columns 32,36,0,0,4,2
```

Two placements cover a checksum domain 0–15, with different overlaps. Under placement A, the nine candidate returns are ABOUT3, GAS768, AMOUNT64 twice, ADVICE32, ABANDON0 twice, ACROSS18, LENGTH1024. The supplied equations preserve only the identity among 7,920 low relabelings and 360 high relabelings. Fixed-weight alternatives leave 10,206 of 63,140,314,380 low layouts and four of 376,992 high layouts. These quantify specificity after choosing the equations. They do not supply the missing operation from checksum nibble to matrix address, or returned scalar to executable CALL. (B:K20–K21; B:K35)

The 183-bit lane makes sixty-one triples: fifty-four `111`, five `101`, and two `011`. Pairing them with the sixty-one occupied canopy cells is a proposed adapter. Across six operations XOR, ADD, and both subtractions change all sixty-one; AND changes two; OR changes forty-two. AND changes occupied rank 28 z→period and rank 58 q→z, raising the ART-projected weight from 37 to 38. But 1,403 of 10,000 same-multiset random arrangements also changed at most two cells and 614 reached weight 38. The sparse effect partly follows from fifty-four all-one masks. (B:K22)

The old reflected-eye word lattice is also preserved: ALERT/AHEAD/CHECK/FAME, the fourth-column RACE, subsequent contextual CHECK trials, and the EXPECT/ALIEN→FINAL→FILE or FINISH branches. Crucially, different predecessors change CHECK results; one cannot take a favorable result from one context and carry it into another. Width 38 explains many diagonal coincidences: among 685 valid offsets separated by 37, 666 yield a down-left diagonal and nineteen wrap a boundary. The visual map needs a selected coordinate rule before arithmetic becomes an instruction. (B:K16; B:K18; B:K21)

## 5. Direct sightlines: the complete boundary and the corrected half-turn

### Why ten, eleven, twelve, and thirteen owners all appear

An optical experiment must state whether a whole character-cell square blocks sight or only the raster's actual ink does; whether observers are cell-centres or arbitrary subcell points; and whether the output records first-hit owners, complete boundary faces, or a projected silhouette. Different models legitimately produce different counts.

The original upper thirty-three-space region, viewed down-left, has a complete twelve-owner boundary `.q,1:q;.1,q:`. An earlier centre-only eleven-owner extraction stopped at `.q,1:q;.1,q` and produced POET / ITEM. Adding the actual final colon at `(16,25)` restores the complete twenty-four-bit record `A72ED8`. Five specified down-left views gave the same twelve-owner boundary. POET / ITEM therefore remains an incomplete-reader observation, not a complete message. ([R1244](#source-r1244); [R1246](#source-r1246))

The lower direct up-right first-hit model has ten owners `:1,q.;q,q.` and twenty bits. Adding semicolon `(18,22)` was incorrect for that first-hit view because period `(18,21)` obscures it. A different whole-cell aperture model includes thirteen owners, including E1 q `(17,11)`, and yields `00111001101010111010011010`: eleven-bit values 461 and 745, then `1010`. Strict complete-record readers reject it. The eleven-character T29 cover is another object again. These counts must remain attached to their precise reader definitions. ([R1246](#source-r1246); [R1395M0004](#source-r1395m0004); [R1726](#source-r1726))

Actual ink adds another population. A frozen official-raster sample, using a 32×32 start lattice in each of the twenty-two lower blanks, returned eighteen owners. Its filtered q/1 channel was `010000000` and punctuation channel `0001101101`, with four z owners retained separately. Their first eight bits equal E’s final bytes `40 1B`, but the complete channels leave `0` and `01`; the match is only two prefixes. Rare hits included one source digit reached by 11/22,528 rays and one q reached by 1/22,528. A literal ten-bit mask over the ten adjacent CONTROL→Z copy edges selected slots 3,4,6,7,9 and values `11101` = 29, not E’s 27. The complete mask was absent from CONTROL and E as a contiguous substring. These are finite sampled-ink results, not a proof of exhaustive positive-area support. ([R1400](#source-r1400))

### The map that preserves the whole observation

The stronger source-to-source alignment is

```text
T30(r,c) = (30−r, 37−c)
```

It pairs five original right-rail ones looking west with five original left-rail q characters looking east, preserving the observer, clear corridor, first obstruction coordinate, and length. The five pairs have 9,7,4,2,0 intervening spaces. The first two obstruction glyphs differ; the last three agree. ([R1726](#source-r1726))

| Right observer | First western hit | Left observer | First eastern hit | Clear spaces |
|---|---|---|---|---:|
| `(12,26)` | comma `(12,16)` | `(18,11)` | period `(18,21)` | 9 |
| `(13,26)` | colon `(13,18)` | `(17,11)` | comma `(17,19)` | 7 |
| `(14,26)` | period `(14,21)` | `(16,11)` | period `(16,16)` | 4 |
| `(15,26)` | comma `(15,23)` | `(15,11)` | comma `(15,14)` | 2 |
| `(16,26)` | colon `(16,25)` | `(14,11)` | colon `(14,12)` | 0 |

Across all thirteen structural ones and twenty-seven q characters, exactly four west/east pairs with nonempty equal clearances produced the same T30 map. Admitting zero-gap rays gives 188 equal-gap pairs and 151 possible half-turns; T30 has eight, the next-highest transforms three. T29 pairs some observers but matches zero complete endpoints. These are bounded geometric counts, not statistical proofs of intention. ([R1726](#source-r1726))

The sixth right-rail one `(11,26)` sees eleven blanks, then period `(11,14)`, semicolon `(11,13)`, q `(11,12)`. T30 sends its observer to `(19,11)`: an unpublished twentieth row. The projection is exact, but there are only nineteen original rows. A proposed Genesis continuation did not satisfy the required eleven-blank window. In contrast, T29 shifts the windows one row, producing cover lengths 2,2,3,2,2 and the eleven-character cover `:1,q.;q,q.;`. That cover's size follows mechanically from misregistration; it is not independent evidence for an eleven-character cipher. ([R1726](#source-r1726))

The paired first hits give right LOW/FULL streams `00100`/`10010`, and left streams `10100`/`01010`. Their literal disagreement flags are `11000`. A modern five-bit alphabet can label this Y or, with reversed polarity, H. No source chooses that polarity or establishes a Bacon cipher. Likewise LOW cannot globally mean “false” and FULL “true”: both sometimes differ at real obstructions.

### The two-eye transfer and the C-field link

The twelve-owner upper wall has a separate coordinate-transfer reading. Under T30, its initial period maps beyond the published sheet to `(19,23)`; retaining the original traversal and taking the eleven on-sheet destinations gives `;.q,q;.q,1:`. LOW/FULL yields `11101001101 11010011100`, or TRUST / SQUEEZE, indexes 1869/1692. Sorting those same destinations into new page order gives `:1,q.;q,q.;`, or CUTE / SNIFF. Including the virtual period gives RISK / INSANE with two residual bits. The direct lower-eye observation cannot see the transferred semicolon behind period `(18,21)`, so TRUST / SQUEEZE is a transported-coordinate result, not what that eye directly sees. ([R1244](#source-r1244); [R1246](#source-r1246))

The terminal upper colon `(16,25)` and lower colon `(14,12)` are the only literal colon-to-colon T30 pair among five original colons. Both sit in original five-glyph `zq:1z` motifs. T30 exchanges q/1 endpoints and retains the central colon and outer z positions. Yet this is part of wider lower-N symmetry: 64/78 eligible five-character windows match under q↔1 reversal versus 42/78 under ordinary reversal. The paired motifs are real; they are not an isolated cryptographic event.

A different whole-picture turn, `G(r,c)=(18−r,37−c)`, maps C’s final printed quartet `w1k=` to source row 16, columns 23–26, read left-to-right as `zq:1`. A/B/E final quartets instead map to `zzzz`. Only C’s printed `1` reflects to a colon. Crucially C’s two Base64 padding bits come from `k`, which reflects to q; the colon reflects to the preceding printed `1`. The colon is therefore not a direct mirror of the padding bits. The two transforms G and T30 cannot be interchanged. The selected `q:1` motifs have parity value 35, tentatively ASCII `#`; Zenon Genesis contains two `#` separators, but upper `.:1` also encodes 35. That recipient remains an added interpretation. ([R1244](#source-r1244))

### The complete DISORDER / PARROT / GIANT stencil candidate

C’s forty ciphertext bits contain three complete eleven-bit words plus seven residual bits. Its first thirty-three bits, `111000000101011101110011110000110`, fit the thirty-three blank cells of the upper cavity. Place one bit in each upper blank in original row-major order, turn that stencil over the lower twenty-two-cell cavity, then read overlap and overhang separately. This is a coordinate projection over printed material, not a collision-free movement of solid pieces. ([R1416](#source-r1416))

All eight lattice orientations and translations were enumerated. Exactly four complete placements cover the lower cavity, all half-turns:

| Stencil transform | Eleven cells outside the lower blank overlap | Output indexes, overlap22 then overhang11 |
|---|---|---|
| `(29−r,35−c)` | `zqzqzq.zqzq` | 471, 519, 813 |
| `(29−r,36−c)` | `q:q,q.;q,q.` | **507, 1283, 781** |
| `(29−r,37−c)` | `:1,q.;q,q.;` | 1277, 1665, 583 |
| `(30−r,37−c)` | All on virtual row 19 | 783, 477, 519 |

The column-36 choice alone includes all five original q-rail sites and adjacent colon `(14,12)` in its overhang. Its complete output is DISORDER / PARROT / GIANT. This connects C, whose authenticated plaintext portion is SMOKE, to the July 25 poem’s hidden giant beneath smoke. The criterion was applied after the result was noticed, despite the rail and colon being known earlier. That historical order prevents calling it a blind prediction.

Reading all thirty-three destination cells in ordinary page order, without separating overlap and overhang, instead gives GIGGLE / DESERT / DOMAIN, indexes 783/477/519. The component-first ordering is therefore a necessary extra rule. Among first/last thirty-three-bit portions of A/B/C/E and all eight thirty-three-bit windows in C, only C’s zero-offset prefix gives GIANT under this stencil. The lower twenty-two projected C bits match the separate field-residual loading at only 12/22 positions. The three-word record is not a standard mnemonic; even a proposed 32-bit-entropy/one-bit-check extension fails, with stored last bit 1 versus required 0. This is a complete reproducible candidate, with no second authenticated plaintext or signature. ([R1416](#source-r1416))

## 6. E feedback, nine-cell completion, and the two rims

The same T30 alignment connects the upper nine-space window at row 12 columns 17–25 with the lower nine-space run at row 18 columns 12–20. It maps comma `(12,16)` to period `(18,21)`, and q `(12,15)` to semicolon `(18,22)`. Among seventeen printed matched lower boundary faces, the comma/period pair is the only mismatch in both LOW and FULL. Comma and period also delimit the original E Base64 field, uniquely among the four fields. This is a precise meeting of geometry and a field boundary; the inherited E 1/E2 dispatch remains a candidate reader. ([R1452](#source-r1452))

The original upper cavity has thirty-two clockwise printed faces. The lower opening has twenty-six boundary faces, seventeen printed and nine beyond the sheet. Under the inherited local E2 interpretation, the upper nine-space window returns to its selecting comma and subsequently to q `(18,11)`. Calling that a self-loop assumes its following cell is still a space. A scratch write changes the input and requires the reader to be recomputed. ([R1452](#source-r1452))

The four decoded fields leave residual lengths 7+1+7+7=22 bits:

```text
A 1011001
B 0
C 1011001
E 0011011
joined 101100101011001001 | 1011
```

Interpreting the first eighteen bits as nine punctuation marks gives `.;:..;:.,`; the final `1011` equals the existing period/semicolon footer. Writing those nine marks into the bottom nine spaces closes the lower opening, leaving thirteen lower blanks and thirty-three upper blanks: forty-six in total. This is the nine-cell insertion experiment, distinct from moving the twenty-two-owner piece. They must not be combined into one state without a separate operation. ([R1452](#source-r1452))

The completed lower perimeter has twenty printed faces and forty bits, `35ab93a3aa`. The original upper perimeter has thirty-two faces and sixty-four bits, `555557ff25eb835a`. The thirteen-byte concatenation did not literally equal ZENON NETWORK; the lower five bytes matched neither C's `e05773c359` nor the original marker stream `8221175310`. The actual OpenPGP test did not identify these rims as a signature, and the sixty-four-bit record did not become a known key identifier. ([R1452](#source-r1452); [R1454](#source-r1454))

The poem overlays are exact but retrospective. The January 14, 2021 sleeping-giant poem has fifty-five whitespace-delimited words without its `$ZNN` footer; mapped to the original fifty-five internal spaces, its last word MAP lands at `(18,20)`. The adjacent original `.;q` maps to binary `101110`, or 46. The July 25, 2021 hidden-giant poem has forty-six words under the same footer convention. After the nine-cell insertion, HIDDEN lands at `(16,15)` beside the other `.;q`, and GIANT at `(17,12)` beside q `(17,11)`. No poem text is literally encoded in the blank cells; loading one word per space is the stated overlay rule. ([R1452](#source-r1452))

At the E2 rail, the scratch insertion changes q+space from `10|01=9` to q+period `10|10=10`, while the printed footer is `10|11=11`. The resulting 9→10→11 progression is preserved as a candidate state relation. Its numerical neatness does not establish a source-written program.

## 7. Moving qzzq: an actual passage, two models of the page

### The minimum physical opening

The four original owners q,z,z,q at `(12..15,15)` can move together by `(+3,−3)` into four original blanks `(15..18,12)`. In the scratch picture this joins upper and lower spaces into one bottom-open fifty-five-space passage. A complete minimum-passage search found eight four-glyph cuts, checked against all 4-of-20 shortest-path-site subsets. Only qzzq could be placed into original in-sheet blanks using that already-retained displacement. This is a meaningful finite selection within the tested movement rule. ([R1475M0001](#source-r1475m0001))

The existing fixed-original-coordinate parity check passes after this move; a live row-major re-enumeration of controls fails. Space and z both have LOW/FULL `01`, explaining why an E1 parity read can remain unchanged even when a moved z physically blocks an empty square. The old E2 window remains, but its `q,` header changes. A result cannot be called “checksum-preserving” without naming the fixed or live roster. ([R1475M0001](#source-r1475m0001))

An experimental fifty-five-byte backing plane retained the entire known envelope across two views: 37+21−3=55 source addresses. Four newly uncovered straight-view bytes were EB28D88A; an E1 window showed `BE59C37357E0`, containing E's first byte and the five C bytes reversed. This observes existing authenticated envelope bytes under an inferred layer model. It does not decrypt a second plaintext. ([R1475M0001](#source-r1475m0001))

### The original sheet and the projected working view

The most important later correction distinguishes the actual nineteen-row page from a T30-projected view containing two virtual rows:

| State | Original nineteen-row page | Projected working view |
|---|---|---|
| Original | Closed 33; bottom-open 22 | Two closed 33 regions |
| Move qzzq | Bottom-open 55 | Closed 66 |
| Move remaining 18 owners by the same offset | Closed 55 | Closed 55 plus virtual-only 11 |

Moving the eighteen owners first gives a projected intermediate 51+15, then the same final state. The order really changes intermediate topology. The projected 66 and 55+11 observations reuse original characters; they are not new inscription rows. ([R1823M0002](#source-r1823m0002))

The joined projected boundary has seventy face encounters and 140 bits, `555557ff25eb83555ae5ab955555e96d556`. Thirteen virtual encounters reuse original owners; the seventy faces represent forty-six distinct original owners. The first thirty faces continue the old upper rim before the path turns beside comma `(12,16)` where the q moved away. Three successive comma encounters still belong to one character. Four anchor/direction readings produced no all-printable seven-bit ASCII; hypothetical 132-bit mnemonic prefixes all failed, with eight remaining bits retained. ([R1475M0001](#source-r1475m0001))

The full twenty-two-owner move fails retained live checks: fixed 9/14 and dynamic 9/6 in the archived sequence. An experimental RETURN restores original appearance; a separately saved observation can retain the known fifty-five-byte record. This gives a concrete meaning to saving state, while leaving the author-selected schedule unproved.

### Changing coordinate frames does not move the mirror

For page position p and a moving-piece anchor a, local position is `u=p−a`. The page-fixed mirror `T(p)=b−p`, where b=(30,37), becomes `T_local(u)=b−2a−u`. With anchor `(12,15)` it is `(6−r′,7−c′)`; after the move to `(15,12)` it is `(−r′,13−c′)`. Keeping the old local formula would accidentally introduce physical displacement `(+6,−6)`. This is an implementation warning, not evidence that the archived replay made that mistake. ([R1823M0002](#source-r1823m0002))

Owner accounting makes the distinction testable. In the piece-attached comparison 179 local coordinates are printed before and after;62 show equal glyphs, but only four retain the same original owner. Fifty-seven z appearances and one q appearance are lookalikes from other source positions. All 370 printed source owners survive the five recorded states. The audit checked 68,265 owner pairs per state and 76 virtual-cell correspondences. On the actual page the relevant boundary has 32→62→32 faces, including eight outside/unknown faces at the midpoint; projection gives 32→70→32. A crop or reflection must not fill unknown originals without naming the projection. ([R1823M0002](#source-r1823m0002))

![An exact physical qzzq move](figures/qzzq_actual_page.png)

*Figure 2. Original characters moved by the declared qzzq operation. The diagram uses the actual nineteen-row page; the separate projected view is explained in the text. ([V01](#v01-editorial-verification))*

## 8. The exact 722-character model and the information it assumes

The October 13 state reconstructed all 418 upper-Z cells but not a satisfactory independent lower-N producer. Perturbing 184 B/C bits affected only twenty-four upper renderer inputs; the other 160 remained authenticated ciphertext. An October 26 composite already reproduced all 722 cells from the Base64 strings plus frozen, previously inferred geometry and C/E marker rules. It also constructed a 42-glyph equal-parity alternative preserving census, occupancy, all 196 observations and the AES plaintext while changing the committed source. The November 4 work quantified the later candidate producer’s output family and dependencies; it was not the first conditional full reconstruction. Both producers were reverse-engineered after seeing the artwork. Exact reproduction establishes a consistent candidate, not recovered 2021 production code. (B:K11; [R1003](#source-r1003); [R1684](#source-r1684))

Within that producer the lower twenty-two markers split into a fixed eleven-marker stem `11101010100`—index 1876, TUNNEL—and a diagonal `00110010000`—index 400, CRAFT. Only six lower assignments vary, from low bits of E[3,5,7,9,11,13]. Their original pattern is `100011`. CRAFT implies the qzzq opening because its first varying q is controlled by E[3]. Thus CRAFT and qzzq are dependent within this model. ([R1684](#source-r1684))

The full candidate census has 262,144 source-control states collapsing to 162 upper outputs ×64 lower outputs=10,368 marker pictures. Selecting CRAFT leaves 162; requiring upper pivot 22 leaves 4; imposing the original q/1 inventory leaves 2; the known CONTROL98 SHA4 leaves 1. The reported 13.340-bit output capacity applies only to this producer. In 8,000 valid alternative GCM encryptions under the public puzzle key and same plaintext, TUNNEL occurs 8,000 times, CRAFT 137, qzzq 4,014, and CRAFT+qzzq 137. A separate 20,000-nonce population gave CRAFT 335 and qzzq 10,084. These controls demonstrate the dependence. Synthetic packets do not carry the original Bitcoin construction signatures. ([R1684](#source-r1684))

### The missing literal-role channel

LOW/FULL merges three character pairs:

| Equal-parity pair | Original positions |
|---|---:|
| z / comma | 224 |
| q / period | 35 |
| 1 / semicolon | 26 |
| **Total** | **285** |

Occupancy and parity alone omit these literal distinctions. ASCII bit 1 distinguishes the members: z has 1/comma 0; q has 0/period 1; digit 1 has 0/semicolon 1. Retaining this plane with the known field bytes reconstructs all original 722 characters. Its 285 positions are an accounting requirement, not proof of 285 deliberately hidden payload bits. ([R1803](#source-r1803))

The frozen contour grammar repairs every one of 285 individual equal-parity swaps and a simultaneous 285-swap synthetic picture back to the original. It accepts six q↔1 changes without repair because those alter marker value rather than equal-parity role. All 10,368 C/E-model outputs are fixed points of that grammar, but the producer supplies 602 of 642 nonfield cells unchanged plus the marker roster. The role plane's invariance is expected: q and 1 both have ASCII bit 1=0. Neither experiment independently derives the entire artwork from a historical logo. ([R1803](#source-r1803))

![Information lost by the two parity readers](figures/parity_information.png)

*Figure 3. The three glyph pairs share their LOW/FULL observations. The additional literal-role bit distinguishes each pair. Counts and the full reconstruction were independently checked for this edition. ([V01](#v01-editorial-verification))*

### The correction from six bits to thirteen free lower labels

Allow the forty marker labels to vary while holding positions, occupancy, punctuation, fields and z fill fixed. Exactly 1,258,815,488 assignments satisfy the fitted contour grammar. For the lower twenty-two positions the grammar forces nine labels and permits thirteen freely varying ones: 8,192 lower pictures. Six are the E-linked sites; seven more were merely held fixed by the old producer:

```text
(14,11) q   (15,11) q   (15,15) q   (16,11) q
(16,26) 1   (17,11) q   (18,11) q
```

Their row-major flags are `0000100`. “Six varying bits” therefore was not a capacity theorem for the original lower artwork. Among the 8,192 grammar-compatible pictures, 534 pass the old four-bit check. Fixing the six E-linked sites leaves 128; adding SHA4 leaves 9; adding the original total lower one-count before SHA4 leaves 7, then 1. These old constraints can select the published state, but they are not newly recovered authentication. ([R1803](#source-r1803))

The subsequent seven-marker reconstruction predicted six rail labels using a whole-edge facing convention from the historical logo, and predicted interior q `(15,15)` from a masked twenty-of-twenty-one-cell rotated tile match. It did not read the withheld labels, use a checksum, or select by word meaning. However the rails and tile relationship had already been studied; this is a masked replay, not blind discovery. Of 352 eligible placements, one matched the rotated tile with zero observed mismatches. Its local transform is `U(r,c)=(27−r,30−c)`, distinct from T30. ([R1808](#source-r1808))

Applying both rail and tile assumptions to the full 8,192 lower family leaves 32 states: five free choices. Rails alone leave 128; rotated equality alone 2,048. This is the current compact calibration family, and its remaining variation matters to the subsequent reader.

## 9. Historical logo reconstruction, holdouts, and frame calibration

The archived May 28, 2020 logo is a 939×1499 PNG, SHA-256 `fa8ee856d77d28675c268714904eecc52bb3f06d08fd9753e337937c907b813e`. It is not proved to be the exact input image used for the 2021 artwork. Prior fits used 316 z/blank locations, excluding q/1 and punctuation labels. Early density/registration results and familiar-letter matches were descriptive fits; historical AAlib or converter failures did not, by themselves, isolate authored steganography. (B:Rendering: failed reproduction does not isolate intentional encoding; [R1783](#source-r1783))

A single sampled-alpha-to-glyph palette can reach only 313/394 in-bounds structural positions, leaving 81 mismatches. Whole-cell coverage can explain more: selected ones `(13,17),(15,22)` cover 51.25% and 49.44%; selected q positions `(14,19),(16,24)` cover 70.44% and 68.12%. The q coverage exceeds the one coverage across 16,618 accepted registration perturbations and five window scales. That supports a local ordinary-rendering explanation. It does not supply a universal rule. ([R1780](#source-r1780))

The next audit found the full six-site contour `q q 1 q 1 q` has coverage 53.69%,32.99%,51.25%,70.44%,49.44%,68.12%. No single threshold separates all six. A five-feature leave-one-row-out classifier predicted 34/40 markers versus a 27/40 all-q baseline, but failed both anomalous ones. Upper-only training predicted 12/22 lower labels; lower-only training predicted 14/18 upper labels. Punctuation prediction was 7/30, worse than the 11/30 majority baseline; cross-half prediction was 4/15 and 2/15. The earlier claim that cell coverage simply explains the anomalies was therefore narrowed. ([R1783](#source-r1783))

The repeated lower punctuation staircase `;.,:` under translation `(+3,+7)` is exact. Across 35 tested positive translations it is the only full-alphabet repeat in the original. But 9,452/100,000 global multiset-preserving shuffles and 28/144 row-preserving permutations also contain one. Historical default-palette simulations reached at most 120/394 structural matches, 2/40 q/1 and 5/30 internal punctuation. A later 48-configuration execution through installed AA-lib reached best 122/414 structural matches—all blanks in that configuration—versus 124/414 for an all-blank baseline; separate maxima were 3/40 markers and 2/58 punctuation. These bounded negatives do not exclude custom palettes, other source revisions, or other production software. ([R1783](#source-r1783); [R1803](#source-r1803))

### What DEVELOP FRAME LOCK actually measures

The lower artwork contains repeated 3×7 tiles. Tile A rotates to tile C exactly; tile B differs at two q/1 sites; another tile is twenty-one blanks. Their common-orientation marker records are 001→010→001. The appealing three-word interpretation DEVELOP FRAME LOCK therefore reuses a reflected state; it is not three independent instruction records. Source-order seven-symbol stencils are `q;.q,1:`→908 and `q;.1,q:`→936, with difference 28 and XOR 36, exactly two changed bits. Mapping 28 to the outer frame requires an additional rule. ([R1762](#source-r1762); [R1766](#source-r1766); [R1780](#source-r1780))

The marker-only frame audit selects T30 uniquely among 3,040 half-turns from the twenty marker locations in original rows 12–18, pairing 18/20 into nine pairs. Across 133 reflected cell pairs occupancy agrees in all and literal glyphs agree in 121, leaving twelve literal differences. The four non-marker ordered differences z→q, q→semicolon, comma→period, colon→comma increase by one under the old code. The full directional difference stream is `111001010011`. ([R1795](#source-r1795); [R1800](#source-r1800))

The later census explains its limits. All unit-step differences constrain only the two orphan marker labels to be q; the other eighteen labels are unrestricted by that property. Among 2²⁰ assignments, 2¹⁸ satisfy it. Preserving each row's marker counts leaves 288 pictures: 144 have all unit changes, 48 also have twelve literal differences, and 4 retain the full sign stream. ([R1800](#source-r1800))

Enumerating 1,536 code maps leaves four satisfying the geometric constraints; two pass CONTROL4. Only the original among those two admits the declared affine ASCII-parity model. Sixty-four whole-byte affine extensions still fit. The other checksum-passing nonlinear map produces sign value 19 and a six-bit digest prefix 26, while the original digest prefix is 38. All are artwork dimensions. This is a useful warning: several retrospective dimension coincidences can coexist. The non-affine alternative is incompatible with the chosen affine family, not cryptographically disproved as every possible encoding. ([R1800](#source-r1800))

Under the six-bit C/E producer the original sign stream fixes `100011`; 8,192 valid alternative GCM packets yield 129 exact original sign streams and seven of those also pass CONTROL4. Deterministic dependence does not make a value meaningless—an author could intentionally choose a nonce to obtain a picture—but it prevents counting the tag, its glyphs, and derived signs as independent evidence.

## 10. FOLLOW GOLD, the leftward eyes, and the thirty-two-state result

The original October 26, 2021 poem ends “Always trust in following one’s eyes.” Its footer is `$ZNN 👀`. In the preserved 148-record export, 138 records are authored by the account and this is its sole authored EYES emoji occurrence. Earlier fifty-word counts legitimately omit the footer, but visual-clue analysis should retain it. The official Twemoji v13.1.0 EYES asset predates the poem: both pupils sit 2.234 units left of their eyeball centres. That supplies a concrete candidate leftward cue, subject to the distinction between an available 2021 asset and the author's actual client display. ([R1812](#source-r1812))

A literal leftward run does not complete the old reader. The last q `(18,23)` encounters `;.`; the last structural 1 `(16,26)` encounters `:qzzzzzq;.`; the unmatched upper right 1 sees an eleven-space gap then `.;qzzzzz,`. An earlier walk that produces `;.,:` looks west, west, then east. The emoji does not authorize that third turn. ([R1812](#source-r1812))

The complete FOLLOW GOLD candidate is explicit: select the bottom-open 22-space component; reflect it horizontally with H(r,c)=(r,37−c); take its forty-cell union; load original forty q/1 bits row-major; XOR reflected partners at original chamber positions; read rows top-down but each row right-to-left. This returns `01011010101 01100100010`, indexes 725 and 802: FOLLOW GOLD. Ordinary left-to-right output yields ONE EYE. These outputs were already recorded on October 20; the November 5 audit replays them. ([R1815](#source-r1815))

The full blank-component census finds sizes 148, 149, 33, 22. Among twelve specified component/reflection combinations, only lower 22 under horizontal reflection gives union 40. Testing 8 source traversals × 8 loading traversals × 8 output traversals × 16 Boolean functions gives 8,192 complete protocols. Four produce the pre-existing FOLLOW GOLD bits. They are one mirror-equivalent construction: their input/loading changes cancel. All use XOR and the same output direction. The target-conditioned census supports a compact description, but cannot retroactively prove the poem prescribed XOR or scratch relocation. ([R1815](#source-r1815))

The new thirty-two-state test is more discriminating. All 32 calibrated lower pictures produce 32 distinct complete outputs of binary difference rank 5. Thus the reader retains all five remaining model choices; FOLLOW GOLD is not forced by silhouette, rails and rotated-tile calibration alone. The original is the sole member giving that target. However the broader forty-bit XOR memory has 2²⁰ preimages for any exact pair-parity result. Preserving thirteen ones leaves `2^9 × choose(11,2)=28,160`. The narrow uniqueness and broad ambiguity are consistent because their constraints differ. ([R1815](#source-r1815))

The proposed NASA check `725 AND 802 = 512` is not unique: changing q `(16,24)` to 1 within the same 32-state family yields 725 and 514, still AND 512. That synthetic picture is not an alternate signed artwork; it demonstrates that the external number 512 cannot alone select GOLD. The old top-left-clockwise spiral gives PICTURE RELIEF; the complete eight corner/direction results are retained below, and the variants initially moving left do not yield that phrase. NASA explains actual record playback, a picture-start signal, vertical raster reconstruction and aspect calibration. It does not specify this chamber, XOR, eleven-bit dictionary segmentation, or spiral start. No 512-line source, new image, or complete downstream message has emerged. ([R1815](#source-r1815))

| Rectangular outside-in traversal | Complete eleven-bit index pair |
|---|---|
| Top-left clockwise | 1314, 1450 — PICTURE / RELIEF |
| Top-left counterclockwise | 1348, 1365 |
| Top-right clockwise | 1162, 1366 |
| Top-right counterclockwise | 674, 725 |
